**11.8 Templates from number theory, carried with their verdicts** *(restored from v2.0 §8.3; dropped in v2.1 — §23.2, item 61).* Two rigorous objects sit next to the modular programme and are kept as templates, not evidence. The **Bost–Connes system** (1995) is a C*-dynamical system whose partition function is the Riemann zeta function, with a phase transition at inverse temperature β = 1: a unique KMS state above the critical temperature, and below it spontaneous symmetry breaking whose extremal KMS states are parametrized by the Galois group of the maximal abelian extension of ℚ. Its role here is exact and limited: it is a worked case of *classical structure emerging as the order parameter of a phase transition in a modular (KMS) system* — the shape A2/A3 would need — and equally a cautionary boundary, since no connection from this template to physical time is known. The **Connes trace formula** (1999) reformulates the Riemann hypothesis as a spectral statement about a scaling action on the adèle class space, a type III modular object; it is cited for the depth of the operator-algebra–number-theory interface and carries no load. Neither enters any work package.

**11.9 The prime-spectral manuscript (clue 6b), audited** *(added 15 October 2026; §23.2, items 63–66; full review `v2.2/assessments/prime-spectral-review.md`; checks `v2.2/verify/primon_checks.py`, 35/35).* A sixth user-supplied document — *The Prime-Spectral Framework: Theorems, Conjectures, and Numerical Tests*, from the same circle as Rev. 2.8 — rebuilds a "primon" cosmology (the bosonic Fock space over the primes, H = ħω₀ log N̂, partition function ζ(βħω₀), Hagedorn pole at σ = 1) in the register-or-demote style this document uses. Its place here is the σ > 1 half of the Bost–Connes template above, and it is kept with the same verdict: template, not evidence. What was verified: the theorem layer is correct and elementary — the Fock isomorphism, Z = ζ, the log-count identity with its exponentially small defect, Hardy–Ramanujan/Erdős–Kac for the *uniform* measure (under the zeta measure the number of prime factors has finite mean Σ_p p^{−σ}, as the manuscript's own ensemble remark says), the no-exact-return theorem (support in the powers of one integer), Bohr almost-periodicity with a Dirichlet return-time bound, Pólya transience for d ≥ 3 modes, the zeta clock (kernel ζ(2ν ∓ iω₀Δτ)/ζ(2ν), Bochner-positive, Lorentzian near the pole, ⟨H⟩·Δτ_{1/2} → ħ), the two-mode sector in closed form. Three small corrections and one citation (Hartnoll–Yang is *The Conformal Primon Gas at the End of Time*, arXiv:2502.02661, 2025 — and it yields automorphic L-functions and Hecke-twisted primon gases, not Z = ζ; §11.10) are listed in the review. **What bears on this document.** (i) The manuscript proves, for its own clock, what Proposition 11.1 argues in general: an almost-periodic kernel carries no arrow, so *the arrow cannot live in the clock* and must be supplied by interaction plus a boundary condition; the "Past Hypothesis as arithmetic necessity" slogan of clue 4 is withdrawn by the manuscript itself in favour of "localized to one distinguished state, conjectural" (its C7) — which is Prop. 11.1's contingent low-entropy state. Recorded as independent support, not as a result of the plan. (ii) Its Page–Wootters section is kinematical only (non-orthogonal clock states, constraint unsolved); its sentence "all incompatibilities of principle dissolve at the kinematical level" is the relabeling this document declines (§23.2, item 16), and it contradicts the section's own disclaimer. (iii) Its conditional Bekenstein–Hawking theorem is circular — the assumed bound E_max(A) = ħω₀A/4ℓ_P² *is* the area law divided by T_H — and the deeper mismatch is thermodynamic: a Hagedorn gas has S ∝ E and infinite heat capacity at T_H, a black hole has S ∝ E² and negative heat capacity; a universal ω₀ cannot equal 2k_BT_H^{BH} for all masses (the manuscript says so). **The numerical result, re-examined.** The manuscript's central negative finding — "no sector ladder crosses the Haar line, so the thermalization conjecture C4 settles on the obstruction side" — is measured against the wrong null. Its ladders are few-mode bosonic sectors with f = d − 1 classical degrees of freedom on ℂP^{d−1}; for such systems the semiclassical theory (Feingold–Peres 1986; Eckhardt et al. 1995: variance of eigenstate expectation values ∝ 1/T_H ∝ ħ^{f−1}) predicts eigenstate fluctuations κ ∝ n^{−(f−1)/(2f)} — 0 for d = 2 (its "analytic floor" is f = 1), 1/4 for d = 3, 1/3 for d = 4, 3/8 for d = 5 — and the many-body D^{−1/2} only as f → ∞. Moreover, in d = 3 and d = 4 its fixed-coupling windows are not quantum-chaotic (⟨r⟩ ≈ 0.13–0.20 and ≈ 0.41 against the GOE value 0.531; reproduced here, with a 7–26% population of near-exact S₃ doublets from the isotropic kinetic term), so ETH makes no prediction for them; Eckhardt et al. predict exactly the slower decay seen in mixed systems. Only d = 5 is a test, and there the measured exponent (0.20–0.26 ± 0.08–0.10) is within one to two standard errors of the correct benchmark 3/8. Re-graded: C4 untested in the ETH regime for d ≤ 4, compatible with few-body ETH at d = 5; the proposed "obstruction theorem" would be a statement about ℂP^{d−1} semiclassics, not about arithmetic. Its Chebotarev branching-ratio test is correctly executed and correctly negative, but the hook is ill-posed (Chebotarev densities count primes; branching ratios are dynamical), does not discriminate from Lie unification (which also gives rationals such as sin²θ_W = 3/8 at the unification scale), and its Higgs "flag" fires only because the group order was allowed to run to N ≈ 3700 against a registered range of 5–360. **Rule.** Every physics claim in the manuscript is a candidate ([H]/[P]) under rule "candidates only"; nothing from it enters Part I, and its register-and-falsifier discipline (A/B/C grades, mathematical vs physical falsifier typing, pre-registration, replicate controls) is adopted for §19.1 as method.

**11.10 "The bridge is the adele" (clue 7), sorted** *(added 15 October 2026; §23.2, items 68–72; review `v2.2/assessments/adelic-bridge-review.md`; checks `v2.2/verify/adelic_checks.py`, 12/12, on Odlyzko's first 100,000 zeros).* A seventh document — a chat transcript in which a model, pressed for "a coherent, unifying image", produces an "arithmetic crystal": ℚ at the centre, the completions ℝ (= general relativity) and ℚ_p (= quantum mechanics) as its leaves, the product formula ∏_v|x|_v = 1 as "the unification", ζ as the crystal's partition function, its zeros as "resonance frequencies", the critical line as "the event horizon", RH as "unitarity", and black-hole entropy as "the count of primes below e^{A/4}". This is the informal layer that the clue-6b manuscript demotes, and it is sorted here the same way. **Theorem-grade and verified:** the product formula (unique factorisation restated); the completed functional equation ξ(s) = ξ(1−s), with the real place supplying the Gamma factor; the Freund–Witten adelic identity A_∞ ∏_p A_p = 1 for Veneziano amplitudes (an identity by analytic continuation, not a dynamics); the explicit formula, under which the zeros *are* the oscillation frequencies of the prime-counting function (reproduced to 10⁻³ at x ≈ 10³ with 10⁵ zeros); and the Montgomery–Odlyzko GUE statistics of the zeros (⟨r⟩ = 0.611 on zeros 10⁴–10⁵ against GUE 0.600, GOE 0.531, Poisson 0.386, with the known finite-height excess decreasing with height). **Half-right:** the Hartnoll–Yang result (verified abstract) maps BKL dynamics to a billiard on half the modular fundamental domain whose quantum states are odd Maass forms; each state's L-function is the partition function of a *Hecke-twisted* primon gas and the wavefunction vanishes at *that* L-function's zeros — not Julia's gas, not Z = ζ, not ζ's zeros; "Kasner exponents are logarithms of primes" occurs nowhere. Sierra's Rindler–Dirac model (2014) is a realisation of the Berry–Keating picture, not a derivation from gravity. **Crossed spectra:** the modular billiard's own energy levels are Poissonian — arithmetic quantum chaos (Bogomolny–Georgeot–Giannoni–Schmit 1992; Bolte–Steil–Steiner 1992) — while GUE belongs to the zeros, which in the gravitational model are nodes of the wavefunction, not levels; the manuscript's C5 is contradicted by this, not supported. **False or empty as stated:** "the Hilbert–Pólya operator is the Dirac operator on the adelic quotient — not a mystery" (it is exactly the open problem; Connes' trace formula realises the zeros as an absorption spectrum and is equivalent to RH only with a positivity); "RH = unitarity/no closed timelike curves"; "the real part gives Einstein's equations, the p-adic parts the commutation relations"; "the Big Bang is a real/p-adic phase transition"; three cited "unified frameworks" that an indexed search does not find. **The one structural fact the image misses, and the only one that touches this plan:** ℝ is the sole completion of ℚ that is an *ordered* field (Ostrowski; −1 is a sum of squares in every ℚ_p — Hensel witnesses computed), so before/after, a time orientation and a Lorentzian signature can be hosted only at the Archimedean place; the p-adic sectors are orderless and, in p-adic AdS/CFT, timeless. The arithmetic object is a static spectral identity; the time it can offer is the Bost–Connes modular flow — thermal time, Branch B of D-14 — and, like the primon clock of §11.9, it carries no arrow (Prop. 11.1 for the third time). Steelman: there *is* one rigorous object — Ostrowski + product formula + Tate + Weil's explicit formula + Connes' adele class space + the BKL/Maass-form dictionary — and it is a coherent picture of **number theory**; it becomes physics only where a system is proved to obey it (the BKL billiard as a model [H]; Bost–Connes and p-adic AdS/CFT as templates [P]). One falsifiable number falls out of the slogan: a prime-count entropy carries a −1·log(A/4) correction (the integer count of clue 6b carries none; one-loop gravity gives ensemble-dependent coefficients) — neither derives A/4, both assume E_max ∝ A. Rule: imagery ([P]) may orient; nothing from it enters Part I.

**In plain terms.** Take an algebra of observables and one faithful state, and nothing else: pure algebra manufactures a flow, fixed by the pair, with the state sitting still while the observables move. In one proven case that flow is the Lorentz boost of a wedge of spacetime, Unruh temperature included. Because the state is a measure and the measure fixes the flow, the Lighthouse's slogan — measure precedes duration — is, here, a theorem. What v2.0 did not have is the news: since 2022 the clock has been put *inside* the algebra together with the gravitational constraint, the algebra's type changes, entropy becomes definable, and in de Sitter space the algebra singles out a state all by itself — the maximally mixed one, the empty universe. That is the far end of time's arrow, not its start; the start must be a contingent low-entropy state, which is exactly what a created order should have and exactly what the plan must still show to be lawful. And one thing v2.0 believed is now wrong: swapping clocks is not harmless bookkeeping. In these algebras, a different clock is a different algebra with a different entropy.

## §12 The measure-first layer (new in v2.1)

The Lighthouse's central physical heuristic is *measure precedes duration* (v1.0 §3; [H]). v2.0 had no place for it: under non-duality a preferred measure looked like a preferred subsystem and was filtered out. Under the real distinction it is admissible, and the audit found that it is not merely admissible but already true in three exact senses. This section states them with registers, states the conjecture that joins them, and turns the fork they open into a prediction.

**12.1 Three exact instances.**

1. **A Lorentzian metric is causal order plus a volume measure (M3) — theorem.** For past- and future-distinguishing spacetimes the causal order determines the topology, the differentiable structure and the conformal class of the metric (Hawking–King–McCarthy 1976; Malament 1977). What the order does not fix is one function: the conformal factor, equivalently the volume element. In causal set theory this becomes the slogan *Order + Number = Geometry* (Bombelli–Lee–Meyer–Sorkin 1987): the order is primitive and the count of elements supplies the volume. And duration is literally recovered by counting: the proper time between two related events is estimated by the length of the longest chain between them (Myrheim 1978; Brightwell–Gregory 1991). *Duration comes from order plus a measure of the M3 kind.*
2. **A clock requires a scale (M3) — theorem, as a statement about conformal geometry; established, as a statement about clocks.** Null geodesics and causal order are conformally invariant; proper time along a timelike curve is not. A world with only conformal structure has order and no duration — which is exactly the premise of conformal cyclic cosmology's crossover and the reason massless fields cannot serve as clocks. The first clock is mass: a particle of mass *m* is a clock of frequency *mc²/ℏ* (de Broglie; made into a working clock by Lan et al. 2013). In the canonical theory the same fact appears as a conjugate pair: York's extrinsic time, proportional to the mean curvature *K* — the local fractional rate of change of the volume element along the normal — is canonically conjugate to the volume density √h (York 1972). In unimodular gravity the cosmological constant is conjugate to a time that *is* four-volume (Henneaux–Teitelboim 1989; Unruh 1989). *Duration is conjugate to volume.* This is the conjugate-pair test of §4 passed, exactly, by the pair measure ↔ duration.
3. **A state (M2) determines a flow — theorem (§11.2).** A faithful normal state on a von Neumann algebra determines its modular flow; the change of state is the Connes cocycle; on a commutative algebra the flow is trivial. *A noncommutative probability measure determines a time.* That this time is physical time is the thermal time hypothesis — conjecture.

**12.2 The conjecture that joins them [H → conjecture].** The three instances use two different senses of measure, M3 in the first two and M2 in the third. The plan's organizing bet is that in the fundamental description they are one structure. This is not free speculation; there are four places where M2 and M3 already fuse in exact results, and the fourth is new:

- *Bekenstein–Hawking and Bousso:* entropy — an M2 quantity, the logarithm of a count of states — is bounded by, and for horizons equals, an area in Planck units — an M3 quantity (D-11's interface bound).
- *Ryu–Takayanagi 2006:* in holographic theories the entanglement entropy of a boundary region (M2) equals a minimal bulk area (M3).
- *Jacobson 1995, 2016:* the Einstein equation follows from δQ = T δS with S ∝ A on local causal horizons; or from the requirement that the vacuum maximize entanglement entropy at fixed volume. A relation between an M2 and an M3 quantity *is* the field equation.
- *The crossed product (§11.4):* on the clock-dressed type II algebra the generalized entropy A/4G + S_out is the entropy of a state with respect to the algebra's trace. The trace is an M2 object — the unique (up to scale) tracial weight — and it carries the area term. *The M3 measure appears as the M2 measure of the gravitational algebra.* That is the strongest current instance of the conjecture, and it was obtained by adjoining a clock, which is the heuristic's own prediction of where it should appear.

Stated: **Conjecture M.** *In the fundamental description the volume measure of spacetime (M3) and the state (M2) of the gravitational algebra are the same structure — the trace of the clock-dressed algebra — and physical duration is its modular flow.* Register: conjecture. Status of the parts: the trace exists and carries area (theorem, semiclassical); that its flow is physical time (thermal time hypothesis, conjecture); that nothing else is needed (open; G19). If Conjecture M holds, the Lighthouse's open questions P2 (how time emerges) and P3 (which measure) are one question, and the clock ambiguity of §9 *is* the state ambiguity of §11 *is* the measure ambiguity. If it fails, the heuristic survives in its two exact M3 forms and the plan loses its unifying thread, not its results.

**12.3 The time fork — D-14.** The two exact M3 instances are *preferred-time* forms: York time is a foliation; unimodular time is a global four-volume. The M2 instance is a *relational* form: the modular flow is a flow of the state, not a foliation. The measure-first heuristic therefore does not choose between the two branches of the oldest fork in the subject:

- **Branch A — covariant/relational.** No preferred foliation; time is recovered from correlations with physical clocks (Page–Wootters; the Trinity; the crossed product; thermal time); preferred clocks are approximate, contextual, and semiclassical.
- **Branch B — preferred time.** A physical foliation or global time variable exists at the fundamental level: constant-mean-curvature slicing and York time; shape dynamics (Barbour; Gomes–Gryb–Koslowski 2011), which trades refoliation invariance for spatial conformal invariance; unimodular time; Hořava–Lifshitz gravity; the foliation of causal dynamical triangulations; the "time of birth" of causal-set sequential growth. *(Status, tenth amendment:* pure shape dynamics now has a fully scale-free formulation (Farokhi–Koslowski–Naranjo 2025, preprint); the causal-set "time of birth" still lacks a quantum dynamics, and the obstruction is sharper — for the two natural operator orderings the quantum Bell-causality condition forces the transition-operator algebra of quantum sequential growth to be commutative, i.e. classical (Srivastava–Surya 2026, preprint).)

v2.0 excluded Branch B on metaphysical grounds. v2.1 may not (§8), and item 19 of §6 says what a Branch-B theory owes: a *derivation* of its preferred time. The fork is open (P2, restated), and the heuristic makes a prediction across it:

> **D-14 (time fork; prediction).** *Whichever branch the final theory occupies, its physical time variable is either conjugate to a volume measure (Branch B: York, unimodular — M3) or the flow of a state (Branch A: modular — M2). Falsifier:* a fundamental theory in which the time variable is a bare parameter conjugate to nothing and generated by no state — an external Newtonian time re-admitted at the foundation — would falsify D-14 and with it the measure-first heuristic. *Discriminators between the branches, none decisive:* Lorentz-violation bounds (constrain Branch B's low-energy footprint; Hořava is already tightly constrained); black-hole interiors, where constant-mean-curvature slicings can fail (a problem for Branch B); the clock-dependence of gravitational algebras (P15 — if the algebra is clock-independent in general, Branch A loses a reason to prefer any clock and Branch B gains none). *Added 15 October 2026 (tenth amendment; §22.13; §23.2 item 100):* the branches are not exclusive at the level of constructions. In JT gravity the constraint generating York-time evolution is adjoined to the subregion algebra by a crossed product, with the extrinsic curvature as the physical clock (Blommaert–Chen 2026, preprint; §11.4) — a Branch-B clock, conjugate to a volume (M3), inserted by the Branch-A mechanism, a flow adjoined to an algebra (M2). It is the first exact toy model in which the two senses of measure in Conjecture M are one object, and it is also a warning: the trace it yields has an entropy with bulk terms, not an area, so the "area term from the trace" (T2′) is clock-dependent — the clock-dependence P15 predicted, now in the form of the entropy rather than its value. D-14's falsifier is unchanged; what it now predicts in addition is that where a final theory has a preferred time, that time will appear inside the algebra as a crossed-product flow, not beside it as a parameter.

**12.4 Register table.**

| Statement | Register | Source |
|---|---|---|
| Metric = causal order + conformal factor (distinguishing spacetimes) | Theorem | Hawking–King–McCarthy 1976; Malament 1977 |
| Order + Number = Geometry; proper time ≈ longest chain | Theorem (kinematic; continuum limit in the Poisson-sprinkling sense) | Bombelli et al. 1987; Myrheim 1978; Brightwell–Gregory 1991 |
| York time conjugate to √h; unimodular time = four-volume | Theorem (canonical GR) | York 1972; Henneaux–Teitelboim 1989; Unruh 1989 |
| Conformal structure gives order without duration; the Compton clock | Theorem / established | standard; Lan et al. 2013 |
| State determines flow | Theorem | Tomita–Takesaki; Connes 1973 |
| S_gen is the entropy of a state relative to the trace of the clock-dressed algebra | Theorem at leading semiclassical order | Witten 2022; CPW 2023; CLPW 2023; JSS 2023 |
| Conjecture M (M2 = M3 at the foundation; duration = its flow) | Conjecture | this document |
| D-14 | Prediction with falsifier | this document |
| "Measure precedes duration" as a first-principles statement (M1) | [H], vocabulary | Lighthouse v1.0 §3; 54:49 |

**In plain terms.** Three things physics already knows: a spacetime is a web of before-and-after plus a rule for how much volume each piece has; nothing can tick unless it has a mass or a size; and in quantum theory a state all by itself generates a flow. In each case something that counts or weighs comes first and duration comes second. The bet of this plan is that these are one fact, and the newest result in the field — that adjoining a clock to the quantum field algebra produces a trace that carries the area of horizons — is the first place where the counting measure and the volume measure are visibly the same object. The old quarrel over whether the universe has a preferred time is left open, but with a prediction that cuts across it: whichever side wins, time will turn out to be conjugate to a volume or generated by a state. If someone finds a fundamental theory whose time is a bare parameter — Newton's, back again — this layer of the plan is wrong.

## §13 The landscape, re-verdicted

v2.0 §8 surveyed the quantum-gravity programmes and §8.4 excluded several — loop quantum gravity, causal dynamical triangulations, causal sets as ontology, preferred-foliation theories — by the non-dual filter ("privileged kinematical graph," "foliation time," "a privileged subsystem"). Those verdicts are withdrawn as verdicts. In their place v2.1 ranks nothing and instead records, for each programme, what it says on the four criteria this document is entitled to use: **D-8** (no fundamental landscape: the state selection must be lawful), **D-9** (no free discreteness scale: any scale must be derived, D-9 being the Lighthouse's bet against a fundamental discreteness parameter), **D-11** (the interface bound must hold), and **D-14** (the time fork). **P9** — whether space is emergent — is open and is *not* used as a criterion; a programme is not marked down for treating space as fundamental, nor up for deriving it.

| Programme | Time | On D-8 / D-9 / D-11 / D-14 | v2.0 verdict → v2.1 status |
|---|---|---|---|
| Canonical / loop quantum gravity; spin foams | Relational (scalar-field clocks in LQC; spin-foam boundary data) | D-9: discreteness of area/volume spectra is derived from the kinematics, not a free scale, but the Barbero–Immirzi parameter is free (a D-9 tension); D-14: Branch A | "Excluded: privileged kinematical graph" → **admitted**; the graph is kinematics, not a preferred clock |
| Causal dynamical triangulations | Foliated (proper-time slicing) | D-9: lattice spacing as regulator, removed in the continuum limit if a second-order transition exists (open); D-14: Branch B unless the foliation is shown to be gauge | "Excluded: foliation time" → **admitted**, Branch B; owes the derivation item 19 requires |
| Causal sets | Order primitive; sequential growth = becoming | D-9: the discreteness scale is a free fundamental parameter (a direct D-9 bet against; the Lighthouse predicts it will be derived or the programme is incomplete); D-11: Order + Number realizes the measure-first structure exactly (§12.1); D-14: Branch B in growth models, A in the kinematics | "Excluded as ontology" → **admitted**; the clearest laboratory for §12 |
| String theory / holography | Boundary time; bulk time emergent (radial direction emergent; §11.5) | D-8: the landscape with anthropic selection is the direct opposite of D-8 — the Lighthouse's sharpest bet; D-11: satisfied by construction (Ryu–Takayanagi); D-14: Branch A | Status unchanged: the strongest formal results and the strongest D-8 conflict |
| Asymptotic safety | Background-dependent effective field theory; time as in QFT | Silent on D-8/D-14; D-9: no discreteness | Unchanged; does not address the problem of time |
| Shape dynamics | York/CMC time, physical | D-14: Branch B, with a stated derivation (conformal superspace; the time variable is the measure conjugate — §12.1) | "Excluded: preferred foliation" → **admitted**; the Branch-B representative that already meets item 19 |
| Hořava–Lifshitz | Preferred foliation, anisotropic scaling | D-14: Branch B; tightly constrained by Lorentz-violation bounds (D-14's main discriminator) | "Excluded" → **admitted, disfavoured on evidence**, not on metaphysics |
| Unimodular gravity | Four-volume time | D-14: Branch B in the mildest form; Λ becomes an integration constant (a D-8 question: lawful or brute?) | New entry |
| Group field theory / tensor models | Relational (condensate cosmology) | D-9: as LQG; D-14: A | New entry; unchanged otherwise |
| Emergent / thermodynamic gravity (Jacobson; Verlinde) | Time as in the underlying thermodynamics | D-11: the field equation *from* the interface bound — the measure-first programme's strongest ally (§12.2) | Admitted, as before |
| Noncommutative geometry (Connes spectral action) | Metric encoded in the Dirac operator; time not addressed | D-9: no free scale; measure-first-friendly (distance from the spectral data) | New entry; interpretation-level relevance to §11.5 |
| Twistor theory | Conformal structure primitive; scale (hence duration) secondary | Realizes "order without duration" (§12.1, instance 2) at the kinematic level | New entry; vocabulary-level relevance |
| Conformal cyclic cosmology | Conformal crossover: no clocks at the boundaries of aeons | Uses instance 2 of §12.1 exactly; its observational claims (Hawking points) are contested | New entry. *The audit's remark associating CCC with 21:104 is struck (§6, item 3; §23); CCC is listed for its use of conformal structure and for nothing else* |
| Gravity-induced collapse (Diósi–Penrose) | External time with objective reduction | D-7 bets against; the parameter-free model is excluded (Donadi et al. 2021); XENONnT 2026 tightens the remaining space ×5 | Unchanged |

**13.1 What the table shows.** Nothing is excluded by metaphysics. Four programmes carry direct bets of the Lighthouse's: the string landscape against D-8; causal sets' fundamental discreteness scale against D-9; collapse models against D-7; and every Branch-B programme owes a derivation of its preferred time. The programmes that sit closest to the measure-first layer — causal sets, shape dynamics, unimodular gravity, thermodynamic gravity, and the crossed-product line within holography — are exactly the ones v2.0 excluded or ignored, which is the measure of how much the non-dual filter cost.

**13.2 Fields-only ontology (v2.0 §9, "Fields of Color").** The reading that there are fields and no particles is an interpretation of quantum field theory consistent with the Lighthouse (the Lighthouse has no particle ontology to defend) and it changes nothing in this plan: it does not supply a time, and it does not solve measurement — the claim that "field collapse" is unproblematic is not accepted (§16). It is retained as vocabulary.

**13.3 Rule for future verdicts [C].** A programme may be marked down in this document only by one of D-7, D-8, D-9, D-11, D-14, by empirical evidence, or by internal inconsistency. "Incompatible with the metaphysics" is not a ground. This closes the route by which v2.0 §8.4 reached its verdicts.

## §14 The hybrid framework: layers and constraints, revised

v2.0 §10 assembled five layers and seven structural constraints. v2.1 keeps the stack, adds the measure-first layer beneath it, moves the crossed product into it as the bridge it is, and rewrites four of the seven constraints where the audit changed their status.

**14.1 The layers.**

| Layer | Content | Status in v2.1 |
|---|---|---|
| **L0 — Measure (new)** | Volume/scale (M3) and state (M2) as the structures from which duration is recovered (§12); Conjecture M; D-14 | Three theorem-grade instances; the join is conjectural |
| **L1 — Causal order** | A partial order of events, continuum or locally finite (the repaired chess analogy: branching order obstructs a single global label) | Kept. *Changed:* discreteness is no longer an "adopted assumption"; it is open, and any discreteness scale must be derived (D-9). Order + Number is the L0–L1 interface |
| **L2 — Quantum reference frames** | Clocks and frames as physical subsystems; covariant transformation of descriptions under frame change (the Trinity; perspective-neutral framework) | Established in finite settings; the algebraic form of a frame change is the crossed product (GL-6) |
| **L3 — Conditional dynamics** | Page–Wootters conditioning; Schrödinger evolution relative to a clock (§9) | Theorem for fixed ideal clocks; non-ideal and dynamical clocks open (G2; WP1) |
| **L4 — State and flow** | Modular flow of the physical state as candidate time; Connes cocycle between clocks; **the crossed product**, which adjoins the L2 clock to the L4 algebra with the gravitational constraint (§11) | Mathematics theorem-grade; thermal time conjectural; crossed product theorem-grade at leading semiclassical order |
| **L5 — Formal shell** | The category-theoretic layer distinction (first-order normalized / higher-order compact-closed / unnormalized), the no-adjoint theorem, the rate–distortion pricing of clocks (§10) | Layer distinction: theorem-grade; C-NOADJ: theorem in finite dimensions (Theorem 10.2, re-derived here), conjecture C-NOADJ′ for algebraic and gravitational categories; the "wall" reading withdrawn by Proposition 10.1 and by the manuscript itself (§10.4) |

v2.0's reading of the stack as "appearances of the one" is replaced by the Lighthouse's: the layers are real, dependent structures at different levels of the created order (§8, gain iii), and the stack is a dependency architecture *about something*.

**14.2 The constraints** (v2.0 Table 10-1, revised).

| # | Constraint | Addressed by | Status in v2.1 |
|---|---|---|---|
| C1 | Order is primitive at the foundation; discreteness, if any, has a derived scale | L1, L0 | *Revised* from "discreteness adopted." D-9 is a bet, not an assumption |
| C2 | Relationality: dynamics relative to chosen subsystems | L2–L3 | Established in finite settings (Page–Wootters; Trinity; QRFs) |
| C3 | Emergence of the Schrödinger equation from conditioning | L3 | Theorem for fixed clocks; dynamical clocks open |
| C4 | Stationary global description | L4–L5 | Constraint form standard (Ĥ Ψ = 0). *Re-grounded:* stationarity of the description is not the ground's priority to time (§6, item 17) |
| C5 | No currying of a nontrivial clock inside a normalized process theory | L5 | *Corrected and now theorem-grade in finite dimensions.* The geometric form fails (Proposition 10.1: the slice adjunction exists, its right adjoint is Newtonian time); the normalized form holds (Theorem 10.2 / Theorem C-NOADJ); the algebraic and gravitational transfer is Conjecture C-NOADJ′ |

The constraint now reads: *covariance where it holds, registered non-invariance where it does not* (P15); selection remains open (§11.6) |
| **C8** (new) | Measure-conjugacy: the physical time variable is conjugate to a volume or generated by a state | L0 | D-14 |
| **C9** (new) | Lawful selection: any preferred state, clock, or low-entropy condition is derived, not posited | L4, §17 | D-8; §6 item 19 |
| **C10** (new) | Absoluteness of outcomes with emergent locality | §16 | G20; forced by the override plus Bong et al. 2020 |

**14.3 What the framework does not claim** (v2.0 §10.3, retained and extended). Not that the intercept principle is proved; not that the thermal time hypothesis is confirmed; not that type III₁ or type II is derived from anything but quantum field theory and the semiclassical constraint; not that space from entanglement is a completed derivation; not that the standard form is more than mathematics; not a solution to the measurement problem; not an origin for the low-entropy condition; not a derivation of the constants; not an explanation of consciousness; and — *new* — not that the real distinction is a physical result, not that any structure here pictures the ground, and not that the problem is dissolved.

## §15 The dependency architecture and the work packages

**15.1 Three senses of solution — now two.** v2.0 distinguished derivation, mechanism, and dissolution, and held only the third "unconditionally available." Under the Lighthouse there are two (§6, item 16): *derivation* (classical time derived from a stationary structure) and *mechanism* (an account of how time emerges from something more fundamental). Both are the plan's business; both are checkable; neither is done. The architecture is a dependency architecture, not a proof chain: it establishes "if A then B," and leaves the absence of the atoms visible.

**15.2 Atoms** (results that must be proved; each a research programme in a specific field). v2.0 Table 11-1 revised; identifiers preserved.

| # | Atom | Field | Status in v2.1 |
|---|---|---|---|
| A1 | The clock-dressed gravitational algebra is well defined beyond leading semiclassical order | Modular theory + gravity | *Restated.* v2.0's A1 ("the modular fixed point is well-posed and reproduces physical self-energy") was half theorem (Tomita–Takesaki) and half non-dual gloss ("self-energy"); the gloss is struck. Leading order: theorem (CLPW; JSS); beyond: open |
| A2 | Locality lemma: the modular Hamiltonian of a region is a local boundary integral | Algebraic QFT | Established for wedges (Bisognano–Wichmann) and conformal spheres (Hislop–Longo); general case open (T1) |
| A3 | The modular flow has a well-defined semiclassical limit | Modular theory + gravity | Open; the crossed product supplies the setting in which to pose it |
| A4 | The Born rule is recovered from the state as measure in the relational setting | Modular theory; QRFs | *Restated.* Gleason/Busch is a theorem (§16); what is open is the conditioning-to-measure map for relational observables (G12) |
| A5 | The renormalization group emerges from modular structure | Modular theory + QFT | Not established; low priority |
| A6 | A massless spin-2 mode in the appropriate limit | Modular theory + gravity | Not established |
| A7 | Connection to Connes's spectral triples | Modular theory + NCG | Not established (GL-1) |
| A8 | The modular flow reduces to the canonical formulation in the non-relativistic limit | Modular theory + QFT | Not established |
| **A9** (new) | A lawful low-entropy condition relative to the trace of the clock-dressed algebra (the repaired arrow atom; replaces T2/G11) | Gravitational algebras + cosmology | Open; the trace is a theorem, the condition is contingent, and D-8 demands its derivation (§17) |

| GL-2 | Modular theory ↔ holography | Modular flow ↔ bulk geometry (JLMS 2016: bulk and boundary modular flows agree in the entanglement wedge) | Established in special cases |

| PH-2 | Derive the constants from the modular structure | Kept, unreformulated; spectral-triple route (T3) is the only candidate |

**15.5 Tractable targets.** T1 — extend geometric modular action beyond wedges and conformal spheres (A2). **T2 — void** as stated ("area law for the modular ground state"; Proposition 11.1); *replaced by* **T2′** — the area term as the entropy relative to the trace for general subregions and beyond leading order (continuing JSS 2023; feeds A9). T3 — exhibit a spectral triple as the modular structure of a specific state (A7 + GL-1). T4 — the Schrödinger equation as the semiclassical limit of the modular flow (A3 + A8). **T5** (new) — characterize when the gravitational algebra is clock-independent (P15; Chen–Penington versus DEHK).

**15.6 Conditional chains** (v2.0 §11.3, revised). *Geometrodynamics:* if A1, A3, A8 hold and GL-1 connects modular to spectral data, A6 follows as a linearized fluctuation, and with A2 plus Jacobson's input the Einstein equations follow as an equation of state. *New alternative route:* trace → generalized entropy (theorem at leading order) → Jacobson 2016 (entanglement equilibrium) → Einstein equations; this route bypasses GL-1 and is the measure-first chain of §12.2. *Probability:* if A4 holds, the projection postulate follows as an approximation to conditioning and the Bell correlations follow without microcausality violation — *with the amendment of G20:* under absoluteness, actualization is nonlocal even though signalling is not. *Parameters:* if A5 holds, the couplings are invariants of the modular data and the Standard Model enters through T3. *Arrow* (new): if A9 holds, the generalized second law (Faulkner–Speranza 2024) gives the arrow in form and the low-entropy condition gives it in fact (§17). Every chain is conditional throughout.

|---|---|---|---|---|
| **WP0 Crossed-product integration** (new) | Absorb the 2022–25 constructions; compute the clock-dependence of algebra and entropy for clock families (monotonic, periodic, bounded-below); test P15 in solvable models. *Scope extended (tenth amendment):* include the intrinsic-clock constructions (Speranza 2025; Freidel–Kirklin 2026) and the York-time crossed product (Blommaert–Chen 2026) — the worked example should be the last, since it is where D-14's branches meet and where the entropy's form (area vs bulk) changes with the clock | GL-6, T5, P15; feeds WP2, WP5 | The leading-order constructions do not extend; clock-dependence is an artefact of the approximation | Year 1: a worked example in which the DEHK entropy difference is computed for two clocks on one subregion; otherwise P15 is demoted to a question about approximations |
| WP1 Relational dynamics laboratory | Exact finite-dimensional clock–system models; non-ideal clocks — including finite-resource *measurement* models, where the ideal-clock idealization fails first (Hausmann–Schmidhuber–Castro-Ruiz 2025; tenth amendment); finite-dimensional (type I) toy versions of the crossed product; state selection in miniature | A4, A8, G2 | Toy models mislead about the type III/II regime | Year 1: a working laboratory with at least one non-ideal-clock correction catalogued |
| WP2 Rate–distortion of clocks | Physical distortion measures — now with a candidate: the entropy difference between clock choices (§11.4) — and R(D) curves for clock families | G4 fill; decoupled from C-NOADJ (§10.6) | The measure may not generalize beyond the semiclassical algebras | Year 1: if no candidate measure survives the toy models, the rate–distortion layer is demoted from pillar to illustration, and the plan says so |

| WP4 Locality and geometric modular action | Extend geometric modular action beyond wedges and spheres (T1); T2′ in place of T2. *Starting point (tenth amendment):* the explicit local-plus-non-local generators for regions bounded on a null plane (Lu–Wang, JHEP 06 (2025) 101) and the deformed-half-space modular Hamiltonians of Ali–Suneeta 2024, where the question is whether the non-local pieces can be moved into the commutant; T2′ carries the caveat that the area form is clock-dependent (§11.4, §12.3) | A2, T1, T2′ | Locality fails in general (itself a publishable result) | Year 3: one new class of regions, or a proof that locality fails there |
| WP5 Semiclassical modular limit | Effective Hamiltonian from the cocycle-perturbed restricted flow; controlled limits; posed on the crossed-product algebra | A3, A8, T4, G9 | The cocycle derivative may not exist | Year 3–4: existence of the effective generator in one controlled regime, or a stated obstruction |
| WP6 Discrete frames | Quantum reference frames on causal orders; the L1–L2 interface; the Order + Number structure as the L0–L1 interface (§12); the rod–clock resolution trade-off for a single space-time QRF (Mattei–Castro-Ruiz 2025; tenth amendment) as a resource constraint | Layer interfaces; D-9 | Finite-dimensional frame theory may not extend | Year 4: a frame-covariant description on one class of causal orders |
| WP7 Phenomenological anchors | Clock-comparison protocols; decoherence records (the conditioning-to-record map of G14); the experimental interface of §7.5; tracking of the collapse-model exclusions (D-7) | G14; C6 infrared; D-7 | No realistic regime separates the models | Continuous; annual update of the D-7 parameter space and the clock-interface experiments |

**15.8 Milestones.** Year 1: WP0, WP1, WP2 deliver a worked crossed-product example, a laboratory, and a first distortion measure. Year 2: WP3's remaining verification completes and C-NOADJ′ is decided for one class of type II algebras. Year 3: WP4 delivers an extension or a negative result. Years 3–4: WP5's effective generator. Years 4–5: WP6 and WP7 deliver the layer interfaces and the experimental update. The priority order of the tractable targets is T5 and T1 first (both now have a literature moving toward them), T2′ second, T4 third, T3 fourth. Failure at any milestone is informative and is recorded in §20, not hidden.

**In plain terms.** Nine components must be manufactured by hand, six connectors must be fabricated (two of them have been cancelled, one has just arrived half-built from other people's workshops), and two wish-list items still cannot be ordered. One component that v2.0 listed as on order — the no-adjoint obstruction — has arrived and been tested, and it is a smaller part than the wiring diagram assumed: a theorem about normalization that says nothing about time's direction. The one genuinely new fact since v2.0 is that the connector the whole diagram was waiting for — the one that joins "clocks are physical systems" to "the state fixes the flow" — now exists in the literature under the name *crossed product*, which is why the first work package is to learn it. What used to be the second-most-tractable target, an area law for a "modular ground state," has been struck, because such a state cannot exist; its replacement asks the same question of the one distinguished state that does exist, which happens to sit at the wrong end of time.

## §16 Measurement, actualization, and the Born rule

The problem of time and the measurement problem meet at one point: quantum mechanics needs a *now* at which the state is defined and probabilities normalized (§7.2), and whatever delivers definite outcomes delivers them *in* time. This section states the Lighthouse's commitments on measurement in their strongest logical form, prices them, and records what the price buys for the time problem.

**16.1 The commitments, in their strongest form.** The Lighthouse holds (v1.0 §4): **D-7**, no collapse — there is no physical process that replaces the unitary dynamics; **D-10**, the Born rule is recovered from the measure structure, not postulated; and the definite outcome is an **I2**-intrinsic fact, absolute rather than perspectival (§8). Logically there are four ways to have outcomes:

1. *Many outcomes* (Everett). Excluded by the I2 commitment: there is one actual outcome. (The Lighthouse's reason is first-principles — facts are known to God singly — and it is entered as a premise, not a result.)
2. *Dynamical collapse* (GRW, CSL, Diósi–Penrose). D-7 bets against the whole family. Status of the bet: live and tightening. The parameter-free Diósi–Penrose model is excluded by the Gran Sasso search for collapse-induced radiation (Donadi et al. 2021); the Majorana Demonstrator (Arnquist et al. 2022) further constrains CSL and Diósi–Penrose parameter space; the XENONnT search for collapse-induced X-rays (1–140 keV, first science run) sets the current world-leading limits — CSL tightened by two orders of magnitude, Diósi–Penrose by a factor of five (XENONnT Collaboration, PRL 136 (2026) 120201); interferometry with large molecules and levitated-nanoparticle proposals press from the other side. *Falsifier of D-7:* a positive detection of a collapse signature — anomalous heating, spontaneous radiation, or interferometric visibility loss beyond all decoherence accounting. This is the testable content the audit asked D-7 to have.
3. *Relative facts* (relational quantum mechanics; QBism). Definiteness relative to a system or agent. **Overridden** (§8): facts are absolute.
4. *Single outcome without collapse*: the state plus something determines the actual value. This is the Lighthouse's position, and it is, by definition, a **hidden-variable theory** in Bell's sense — whether the "something" is a configuration (Bohm), a stochastic process (Nelson), or a measure over actual values (the Lighthouse's own M2 phrasing). (What this position does to *time* is now stated: the pilot-wave row of §7.3 and the trajectory-time row of §9.4 — the trajectory supplies order and duration; the price is a distinguished foliation; the arrow is still not supplied.)

**16.2 The price of position 4 — theorems.**

| Theorem | What it forces on position 4 |
|---|---|
| Bell 1964 (established experimentally; loophole-free 2015) | Nonlocal dependence of outcomes on distant settings |
| Kochen–Specker 1967; Spekkens 2005 | Contextuality: the actual value depends on which compatible observables are co-measured |
| Pusey–Barrett–Rudolph 2012 | Under preparation independence the quantum state is ontic — not merely knowledge of the hidden variables |
| Frauchiger–Renner 2018; Bong et al. 2020 ("local friendliness") | *Absoluteness of observed events* + locality + no-superdeterminism is inconsistent with quantum predictions (and the inequality is violated experimentally in proof-of-principle form — photonic friends in Bong et al. 2020; friends of growing "branch factor" and, in 2026, agent-like friends with memory and conditional action on superconducting processors, loopholes open: Zeng–Labib–Russo, Quantum 9 (2025) 1851; Laux–Cavalcanti, arXiv:2609.12527). With absoluteness held (§8), **actualization is nonlocal**, not merely correlation |
| No-signalling (theorem of the formalism) | None of the above is usable for signalling; the nonlocality is at the level of facts, not of messages |

**G20** is the ledger entry: the Lighthouse's measurement position is a nonlocal, contextual, ψ-ontic hidden-variable account with absolute outcomes. The Lighthouse can pay, because it already holds locality to be emergent (v1.0 §3) and it has no particle ontology (§13.2) — but it must be seen to pay, and no sentence of this document may describe the position as "just quantum mechanics without collapse."

**The I2 trade-off (admitted to the inventory, §4).** The pair *intrinsic (I2) ↔ relational* passes the trade-off test in this form: the more an outcome is treated as context-independent (an I2 fact fixed before the context), the more nonlocality and contextuality the account must carry, and the amount is quantified — by the Bell and Kochen–Specker inequality violations, and more finely by the contextual fraction of Abramsky–Brandenburger 2011, which bounds the share of any empirical model that admits a context-independent explanation. That is an inequality, which is what band 1 of the map requires.

**16.3 D-10: Born from measure.** *Theorem:* on a Hilbert space of dimension ≥ 3, any countably additive probability assignment on projections is given by a density operator (Gleason 1957); for effects (POVMs) the result holds in all dimensions ≥ 2 (Busch 2003; Caves–Fuchs–Manne–Renes 2004). So the Born rule is the *only* noncontextual probability measure on the quantum event structure. What D-10 adds, and what remains conjectural: that the measure in Gleason's theorem is the state of the world in sense M2; that it is the *same* measure whose flow is time (Conjecture M, §12.2); and — for position 4 — that the actual values are distributed according to it. The last has one worked instance: in Bohmian mechanics the Born distribution is derived as *typicality* with respect to the natural measure on configurations (Dürr–Goldstein–Zanghì 1992) — "Born from measure" in exactly the sense D-10 means, with the equilibrium hypothesis as its contingency. Alternative derivations (Deutsch–Wallace decision theory; Zurek's envariance) are recorded with their contested assumptions and are not relied on. A4/G12 — the conditioning-to-measure map in the *relational* setting, where the "now" is a clock reading — is the open atom. *Tenth amendment (§22.13):* the typicality step has its algebraic foundation — the Lubkin–Page bounds hold for all type II factors, including the clock-dressed gravitational algebras, and fail for type III (Wang–Braunstein, PRD 113 (2026) L121904); what remains open is unchanged, the relational conditioning map A4.

**16.4 What the plan may and may not say about actualization.** It may say: outcomes are I2 facts; the measure is the Born measure; the mechanism is unknown; the account is nonlocal. It may not say: that God actualizes outcomes (§6, item 1 — the ground upholds everything and is a mechanism of nothing, rule 7 of §2); that consciousness actualizes outcomes (item 8); that "field collapse" is unproblematic (§13.2); that relational definiteness is enough (§8). The v2.0 glue "relational actualization = the collapse reading" (G14) is kept only as its *physics* component: the conditioning-to-record map through decoherence (WP7).

**16.5 Register table.**

| Statement | Register |
|---|---|
| Gleason; Busch | Theorem |
| Bell; KS; PBR; Bong et al. | Theorem (with their stated assumptions); Bell and local friendliness tested |
| Diósi–Penrose parameter-free excluded; parameter space tightening | Established (Donadi et al. 2021; XENONnT 2026: DP ×5, CSL ×100) |
| D-7 (no collapse) | Prediction; falsifier stated |
| D-10 (Born from the world's measure; one measure for Born and for time) | Conjecture, with the Bohmian typicality instance |
| Absoluteness of outcomes | Premise (first-principles register); cost registered (G20) |
| Decoherence selects pointer bases and records | Established; time-symmetric; not a solution to the problem |

**In plain terms.** The Lighthouse says: one outcome really happens, nothing in nature "collapses" to make it happen, and the odds are the ones the quantum state already carries. Put that way, it is a hidden-variable view, and the theorems of the last sixty years say what such a view costs: the fact of an outcome here depends on choices made far away, depends on what else was measured alongside, and the quantum state is a real thing, not an opinion. The Lighthouse can afford this — it never believed locality was fundamental — but it must say the price out loud. The one thing it gets for free is the probability rule: mathematics shows there is only one consistent way to assign odds to quantum events, and the bet is that this one measure is also the measure whose flow is time.

## §17 The arrow of time

**17.1 Two problems, not one.** The source documents' "arrow specification" ran together two questions that must be separated (audit of clue #2):

- **(a) The low-entropy condition.** Why was the entropy of the early universe so low (Penrose's Weyl-curvature hypothesis; Albert's Past Hypothesis)? Given time-symmetric laws, this condition, not the laws, produces the thermodynamic arrow, and with it the arrows of records, memory, causation and radiation (strong consensus). *Status of the secondary arrows, made precise (15 October 2026, ninth amendment; clue 12):* the **records and memory arrows are derivative at theorem grade** — a trace of the past exists only given an earlier lower-entropy state (Reichenbach 1956; Albert 2000), the thermodynamic conditions sufficient for macroscopic traces are explicit (Rovelli 2022: system separation, temperature differences, long thermalization times — "low entropy transformed into available information"), and *any* physical system that preserves a record, dissipative or not, must have its arrow aligned with the thermodynamic arrow, from robustness of that arrow plus the requirement that a memory not be fine-tuned to what it records (Mlodinow–Brun 2014); the division into past, present and future is a description of how information-gathering-and-utilizing systems process information, consistent with four-dimensional physics (Hartle 2005); and when the universe's entropy dynamics is formalized as a time-symmetric, time-translation-invariant Markov process, every asymmetry of memory, every Boltzmann-brain argument and the second law itself turn out to depend on a *conditioning time* that the process does not supply (Wolpert–Rovelli–Scharnhorst, Entropy 27 (2025) 1227; tenth amendment) — Proposition 11.1's pattern in classical dress. It follows that an argument from the brain's temporal asymmetry — memory of the past, Bayesian prediction of the future, "no encoding mechanism for future events" — to the *illusoriness* of the thermodynamic arrow runs backwards: the asymmetry it cites is an instance of the arrow, not its source (same error family as §23.2 items 78 (D4) and 90). What remains partial is the derivation of the **radiative** and **causal** arrows from the thermodynamic one.
- **(b) The orientation.** Why is there a single, global direction at all — rather than two arrows pointing away from a middle (Gold's two-boundary universe; Barbour–Koslowski–Mercati's Janus point in the N-body problem, 2014; Carroll–Chen's baby universes nucleating in both directions from a high-entropy middle, 2004)? Time-symmetric laws plus one boundary condition yield an orientation only relative to that boundary; nothing in (a) makes the orientation unique. Orientation is not even locally forced: regions with opposite thermodynamic arrows can coexist and interact weakly (Schulman, PRL 83 (1999) 5419). *The live alternative to D-8's reading of (a) (tenth amendment; §22.13):* in the Janus-point line's strongest form, the global spatial scale is treated as surplus structure, the scale-reduced dynamics has universal attractors and Janus points, and an arrow is claimed without time-asymmetric laws *and without a Past Hypothesis* — generic rather than lawful-contingent (Gryb–Friederich 2025, preprint; numerical support for the growth of structural complexity away from the Janus point in Barbour–Lobo–Lourenço 2026, preprint). The plan's position: a generic arrow is still an arrow relative to the Janus point, two-sided; what makes our side ours is a condition on where we are, not on the laws — (a) relocated, not removed. If the relocation is complete, D-8's demand on (a) is met trivially and the plan must say so; registered as the alternative, not adopted.

Under the Lighthouse, (a) is a case of **D-8**: the selection is contingent (which is expected of a created order) and must be *lawful* — derived from a principle, not posited as a brute fact. (b) is split by register. *(Independent support, 15 October 2026 — §11.9, §23.2 item 66: the clue-6b manuscript proves for its arithmetic clock that an almost-periodic correlation kernel carries no arrow, relocates every arrow to interaction plus boundary condition, and demotes its own "low-entropy past as arithmetic necessity" to a conjecture about one distinguished state — which is (a) stated from the other side.)*

*Candidates and falsifiers for (a), carried from the arrow specification (clue 2) with their scope restored (§23.2, item 58).* The specification named three existing proposals for a lawful low-entropy condition, each of which makes CMB-level predictions: the **Weyl curvature hypothesis** (Penrose 1979 — vanishing Weyl curvature at the initial singularity; a specific low-entropy state), the **no-boundary proposal** (Hartle–Hawking 1983 — the initial state selected by a Euclidean path integral; a specific primordial power spectrum, no initial singularity) and the **tunnelling proposal** (Vilenkin 1982 — a different spectrum); a fourth form, stated in entanglement rather than thermodynamic entropy, is the *entanglement past hypothesis* (Al-Khalili–Chen, Found. Phys. 54 (2024); tenth amendment). *Epistemic ceiling on all four (tenth amendment):* typical state vectors in the low-entropy subspace are observationally indistinguishable — no observation identifies, or appreciably narrows, the universal state within it (Chen–Tumulka 2024, preprint) — so D-8, as applied to (a), is a claim about the lawfulness of the *subspace*, not of the vector (G19). Under Conjecture M these are candidate *forms* of A9: a derivation of the low-entropy condition from the measure structure, not a stipulated boundary condition. The specification's falsifiers stand, re-scoped: (i) a final theory in which the initial state is a landscape of environmental conditions falsifies D-8 as applied to (a) — one bet with two consequences; (ii) exclusion of both wavefunction proposals by the CMB with no derivable alternative in sight puts the bet in trouble, not out; (iii) a two-ended, Carroll–Chen-type universe — low entropy at both ends, reversing in the middle — would kill the *orientation* claim, which this plan has already withdrawn from physics (b), and would leave (a) untouched. What the bet still does not give is a number: not which state, not the tensor-to-scalar ratio; it gives the form of the answer — derivable, not arbitrary — and a derivation is the target. In the first-principles register the created order has one direction; that is a revelation claim and may not be entered as physics (§6, items 2, 10, 14). In the physics register (b) is **open**: the Janus-point models show that a single orientation is not forced by the laws, and no result of this plan favours one orientation over two. The plan predicts nothing about (b).

**17.2 The modular correction.** v2.0 proposed that the arrow be the positivity of a modular Hamiltonian in a "modular ground state." Proposition 11.1 shows there is no such state on any type III algebra, and that in the clock-dressed type II₁ algebra the one distinguished state is the trace — *maximum* entropy, the arrow's far end. The arrow therefore enters the modular programme in two pieces: the **generalized second law in gravitational algebras** (Faulkner–Speranza 2024; a local form in crossed products for static and Kerr black holes, Ali–Suneeta 2024, preprint), which is the arrow *in form* — entropy relative to the trace does not decrease along the flow — and the **low-entropy condition relative to the trace**, which is the arrow *in fact* and is contingent. The second is atom A9. The process-theoretic side says the same (§10.7): exact reversal is prior-indexed — the Petz transpose is a functor on *pointed* channels, built from the same relative modular data as the flow — so the arrow is carried by the state, never by the category of processes or by the algebra alone. The plan's bet, which is the Lighthouse's D-8 applied here: A9 is lawful — there is a principle (a measure-first principle, if Conjecture M holds: the initial state is distinguished by the same M2/M3 structure that fixes the flow) from which the condition follows. *Falsifier:* a demonstration that the low-entropy condition is a free parameter of the final theory with no derivation — the brute-fact outcome.

**17.3 The cosmological measure problem.** In eternal-inflation cosmology the probabilities of observations depend on a measure over an infinite ensemble of pocket universes, and no measure is agreed (the Boltzmann-brain and youngness paradoxes are symptoms). This is an M2 problem, and it is the arrow problem's cosmological form. **D-8** makes a sharp prediction here: *there is no fundamental landscape*, hence no measure over pocket universes to choose; the measure problem is predicted to disappear with the landscape, not to be solved within it. If the landscape is real and the measure problem genuine, D-8 fails and with it a central Lighthouse bet (§18). The plan does not hedge this.

**17.3a Laboratory analogues, scoped** *(restored from v2.0 §8.2; dropped in v2.1 — §23.2, item 61).* Two verified results give the intrinsic/extrinsic split and the arrow a laboratory form, and each contributes exactly one scoped observation. In **time-varying media** (Koivurova, Robson and Ornigotti 2023) a wave whose propagation speed changes in time obeys an accelerating wave equation admitting only forward solutions, with time reversal possible only at zero acceleration; the wave carries an intrinsic time distinct from laboratory time, and the acceleration induces a non-stationary effective metric — the sphere analogy of §10.1 realized in classical wave mechanics. Its "microscopic arrow" is a model-specific asymmetry, not a derivation of the arrow. In **monitored quantum systems** (García-Pintos, Liu and Gorshkov 2026) a control Hamiltonian can replicate the stochastic trajectories of a continuously measured system, and feedback built on it can blur, amplify or overcompensate the measurement disturbance, producing dynamics within the controlled subsystem more consistent with time running backward than forward. The scoped observation is operational: at the level of description the arrow's direction is agent-relative and locally reversible, robust in practice; nothing follows for fundamental time. A third analogue was added on 15 October 2026 (§22.10): in an isolated **cold-atom system** with a time-independent Hamiltonian, partitioned optically into a "dark" and a "bright" sector whose effective Hamiltonian is minisuperspace-like, the natural internal clock field is *not* monotonic across the sector's recollapse, whereas an operationally defined *entropic time* — built from the coarse-grained entropy exchanged between the sectors — orders the data monotonically, yields an effective Schrödinger equation that reproduces the measurements, and stops at the sector's "heat death" (Barontini et al., Phys. Rev. Research 8 (2026) L022047). The scoped observation is the ledger's own division (§9.4): an internal clock supplies order and fails where it is non-monotonic; the arrow in the analogue is read off a coarse-grained entropy of a particular state, which is Proposition 11.1's pattern, not a derivation from structure. All three are analogues of the plan's structures, not instances of them.

**17.4 Decoherence and records** (unchanged). Decoherence is time-symmetric; it produces records only in the presence of (a). "Becoming" — whether conditional correlations constitute a real flow — remains excluded from the falsifiable core (§9, open item 5), with causal-set sequential growth as the one programme that offers a mechanism for it.

**17.5 Register table.**

| Statement | Register |
|---|---|
| Thermodynamic arrow from a low-entropy boundary condition | Strong consensus |
| Secondary arrows from the thermodynamic arrow | Partial |
| No modular ground state; the trace is maximum entropy | Theorem (Proposition 11.1; CLPW) |
| Generalized second law in gravitational algebras | Theorem at leading order (Faulkner–Speranza 2024) |
| A9: the low-entropy condition relative to the trace is lawful | Conjecture (D-8 applied) |
| Single orientation (b) | First-principles register: held; physics register: open |
| D-8: no fundamental landscape; measure problem dissolves with it | Prediction; falsifier stated |

**In plain terms.** Why does time have a direction? Two questions hide in that one: why the universe started tidy, and why there is one direction rather than two pointing away from a middle. On the first, the Lighthouse bets that the tidy start was law, not luck, and now knows where to look: the one distinguished state the new algebra offers is the maximally *untidy* one, so the tidy one must be singled out by something else — ideally by the same measure that gives the flow. On the second, physics has no answer and this plan offers none; what revelation says about the direction of the world stays in its own register.

# Part IV — Registers

## §18 Predictions D-1 – D-14

House rules (v1.0 §10, retained): every prediction names what would falsify it; strength is graded; null results are not accumulated as confirmation (§6, item 15) — the collapse watch is scored against a dated forecast (RQ-8). The framework predicts **no** new particle, number, or quantum-gravity signal; it predicts the *shape* of the final theory.

**What this framework does not predict** *(v1.0 §10, restored verbatim in substance — §23.2, item 57).* No new particle, no new force, no new number, no new effect. No specific cosmological signature; no quantum-gravity signal. On the origin of time's arrow it predicts the *form* of the answer to (a) and nothing about (b) (§17.1). It predicts the *shape* of future physics, not its content — which is why its predictions are of the kind "there should be a derivation" and "these should turn out to be one structure".

**Patterns to look for** *(v1.0 §10).* Wherever two complete descriptions of one system compete, look for the axis. Wherever a causation puzzle shows over-determination, look for the missing level. Wherever a bridge claim appears, ask for its independent warrant; the expectation is that most are unwarranted, and the discipline is to say so. Wherever a framework becomes compatible with everything, its discriminating core has been lost; find where it used to bleed. Wherever a dissolution appears, ask what it forbids; a dissolution that forbids nothing is a re-description (§4.2). Wherever a borrowed layer is doing explanatory work, separate assumption from evidence.

**Questions the map makes askable** *(v1.0 §10, with v2.1's one correction).* (1) What is the adjoint of each fundamental construction? (2) Which trade-off inequality or adjunction does each polarity carry? — v1.0 asked "which conserved quantity", and §4 retired that test: conservation follows from symmetry, not from polarity. (3) Where are the dissociation pairs in each claimed polarity (N-5 is the template)? (4) Which open question does a proposed finding fill — and does it meet the fill-conditions, or merely touch the word? (5) What would the absence of the predicted structure look like, stated in advance? (6) Which level is ours (P10), and does any answer explain, or merely absorb, the question? (7) Which dissolution could be turned into a forbiddance — a claim the world could fail (D-7 is the worked example)?

*Neuroscience.*
